# SCOTTSREADME -- vSAN Host Metrics 2.0.0: how the data is structured

this management pack's data is structured so that improvements to the metric

## 1. Purpose and the 2.0.0 design

### How to read this document

* **Entity type** -- a vSAN Performance Service entity type, e.g.
  `vsan-pnic-net`. It is the unit of query (one `VsanPerfQueryPerf` per type)
  and the unit of the metric tree (one `TREE` row per type).
* **Identity parts** -- the named fields of an `entityRefId`
  (`app/perfsvc_model*.py` `ENTITIES[...]["identity"]`).
* **Target** -- the VMware-owned object a metric is written onto.
* **Group** -- the `|`-separated path under `cptvsan` that `TREE` assigns.
* **Instance** -- the `:<instance>` suffix on the last group level. It is
  one of two things, told apart by whether the `TREE` template has a
  placeholder:
  * a **device** (`{vmnic}`, `{disk_uuid}`, `core-{cpu}` ...) -- more than one
    object per target, one instance per NIC, disk, core, world, vSCSI device;
  * a **variant** (`dom-owner`, `vsan`, `local` ...) -- a literal. Sibling
    entity types were merged into one group and the variant names which one,
    e.g. `Storage I/O:dom-client` and `Storage I/O:dom-owner`. There is exactly
    one per target.
  A device template may carry a variant prefix (`CPU:core-{cpu}` beside
  `CPU:vsan`, `vSCSI:remote-{device}` beside `vSCSI:{device}`).
* "**unit authority**" says WHICH source a unit came from (section 6).
  "derived from id" labels are a mechanical camelCase expansion of the metric
  id: they are not an authoritative meaning.
* Tables are generated. Narrative is hand-written inside the generator.

### 2.0.0 in brief

* The pack defines **no object kinds of its own** and **writes no
  relationships**. `adapter.collect()` returns a `CollectResult` whose only
  object is the adapter instance itself, carrying the `Collector|*`
  self-metrics, with `update_relationships = NONE`;
  `_assert_no_foreign_content` raises if any other object or any relationship
  slipped in. (This is the repository's hard rule 1:
  relationship serialisation in the SDK is replace-not-merge, and the 1.x
  attach-to-vCenter design destroyed vCenter's own VM-to-host edges.)
* Every metric is **pushed onto a VMware-owned object** that the built-in
  vSphere adapter (`VMWARE`) already collects -- `ClusterComputeResource`,
  `HostSystem` or `VirtualMachine` -- through the Suite API **batch** endpoints (`app/push.py`),
  chunked by object count and bytes, several chunks in flight at once:

  ```
  POST api/resources/stats
  POST api/resources/properties
  ```

  A chunk the server rejects is re-sent per resource
  (`POST api/resources/{id}/stats` and `.../properties`). `_guard_url`
  refuses any URL containing `adapterkinds` or `relationships`, and every key
  must be under `cptvsan|`.
* Operations resource ids are found by listing `VMWARE` resources of each kind
  (`GET api/resources?adapterKind=VMWARE&resourceKind=<kind>`, paged) and
  matching the pair (`VMEntityObjectID` = MoRef, `VMEntityVCID` = vCenter
  `about.instanceUuid`). The ids are **cached** (`push.IdCache`): a full listing
  only when an entry is older than `id_cache_ttl_minutes` (default 30) or after
  a 404, one `recentlyAdded` delta listing per kind per cycle for a MoRef not
  yet seen. A sample of pushed ids is re-read every cycle to catch a vanished
  object.
* **Prefix**: `cptvsan`.
* **Key grammar** (`metric_tree.build_key`):

  ```
  cptvsan|<group>[:<instance>]|<metric>
  ```

  `<group>` is the `TREE` group path (may itself contain `|`, e.g.
  `Network|Physical NIC`); `:<instance>` is appended to the LAST group level
  only for entity types whose `TREE` row has an instance template; `<metric>`
  is the Performance Service metric id verbatim (`metricId.label`).
  Example: `cptvsan|Network|Physical NIC:vmnic0|rxMissErr`.
* **Instance sanitising** (`sanitize_instance`): `:` and `|` in an instance
  value are replaced by `-`. Nothing else is changed: `/`, spaces, dots and
  parentheses pass through. So `scsi0:0` becomes `scsi0-0`, an iSCSI IQN
  loses its colons, and a virtual-disk path keeps its `/`.
* **Freshness**: every resolved target also receives the property
  `cptvsan|Collector|lastUpdated` = collection timestamp in epoch milliseconds (string),
  so a stale target is visible from its own properties.
* **Labels and units are NOT sent to Operations.** The stats/properties push
  carries no metadata; pushed keys have no describe.xml entry. The labels and
  units in `app/metric_labels.py` are used by the content generators
  (`tools/build_view.py` puts the unit symbol into the column label). In the
  Operations metric picker a pushed key shows as its raw key path.

## 2. The vSphere JSON API path end to end

Since 2.0.0 the adapter speaks only the vSphere JSON API (vCenter 8.0 Update 3
or later, or 9.0 or later): `app/vcenter.py`, plain HTTPS with `requests`, no
SOAP library. Request and response shapes follow the MIT-licensed reference
pack vcf-content-factory-sdk-vsanperf (Scott Bowe), see NOTICE. One collect is
`app/collector.py` `run_collection`.

### 2.1 Calls per collection cycle

| # | Endpoint (`/sdk/vim25/8.0.3.0/...`) | Purpose | Code |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `GET SessionManager/SessionManager/currentSession` (saved `vmware-api-session-id`); `POST .../Login` only when that is empty | One session REUSED across collect processes (its id is kept in the state file, 0600). | `vcenter.VcClient` |
| 2 | `GET ServiceInstance/ServiceInstance/content` | `about.instanceUuid` = `VMEntityVCID`; `about.version`/`build` = the support floor (Test Connection fails below 8.0 U3). | `VcClient.check_version` |
| 3 | `POST PropertyCollector/<own pc>/WaitForUpdatesEx` | Inventory (clusters, hosts, VMs) from a session-lived filter: only CHANGES after the first cycle. A full read on first run, new session, fault, or hourly. | `inventory.InventoryTracker` |
| 4 | `POST HostVsanSystem/<id>/QueryDisksForVsan`, `vsan/.../QueryVsanManagedDisks`, `RetrievePropertiesEx` (storage), `vsan/.../VsanQueryVcClusterHealthSummary` | Disk -> host, disk names and hardware. Cached for an hour per cluster. | `perfsvc.disk_inventory` |
| 5 | `POST vsan/VsanPerformanceManager/vsan-performance-manager/VsanPerfQueryPerf` | **One call per entity type per cluster** (vSAN refuses two wildcard specs in one call: `...queryperf.wildcardlimit`), with the `labels` filter, gzip. HEAVY types (`app/cadence.py`) every 3rd cycle. | `vcenter.query_cluster` |
| 6 | Suite API `GET api/resources` (TTL 30 min, `recentlyAdded` delta on a miss); `POST api/resources/stats` and `.../properties` (batch) | Resolve and push. | `push.IdCache`, `push.BatchPusher` |

### 2.2 `VsanPerfQueryPerf` request

```json
{"querySpecs": [{"_typeName": "VsanPerfQuerySpec", "entityRefId": "vsan-pnic-net:*",
                 "startTime": "2026-11-07T02:15:00Z", "endTime": "2026-11-07T02:30:00Z",
                 "labels": ["rxCrcErr", "rxMissErr", "..."]}],
 "cluster": {"_typeName": "ManagedObjectReference", "type": "ClusterComputeResource",
             "value": "domain-c1"}}
```

* **Window.** From the newest sample already pushed for that (cluster, type)
  to now: about 5 minutes for CORE types, 15 for HEAVY, at most 60 after an
  outage (`cadence.window`). Every sample in it is pushed with its own
  `sampleInfo` timestamp; a sample at or before the last pushed one is skipped.
* `InvalidArgument` naming the entity type, or `NotSupported`, means "not
  available on this cluster": remembered for 6 hours, summarised once.

### 2.3 `VsanPerfQueryPerf` response, exactly as consumed

A JSON array of `VsanPerfEntityMetricCSV`, one per object of the type:

| Field | Used by the code |
|---|---|
| `entityRefId` | **Yes.** Entity type + identity parts (section 3). A ref containing `*` is the query echoed back for an empty type and is skipped. |
| `sampleInfo` | **Yes.** CSV of UTC `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS`, one per sample; each value is pushed at its own timestamp. |
| `value[].metricId.label` | **Yes** -- the metric id, the last key segment |
| `value[].values` | **Yes** -- CSV aligned with `sampleInfo`; `None` = no sample (absent, never zero) |

### 2.4 From response to rows (`perfsvc.RowSink` -> `push.plan`)

1. `parse_ref` splits the ref into entity type and identity parts. Missing
   trailing parts are filled with `(all)` (host-cpu's per-host aggregate
   becomes the instance `CPU:cores-total`); surplus parts are kept as `part<i>`.
2. Per series: skip `<name>Actual` twins; keep every numeric sample newer than
   the last pushed one; divide by 10 if the id is in `perfsvc.PERMILLE`.
3. `push.plan`: resolve the MoRef (section 4); build keys with
   `metric_tree.build_key`; derived metrics per sample timestamp; `name` and
   disk-hardware properties for `vsan-esa-disk-layer`, `vsan-esa-disk-scsifw`, `cache-disk`, `capacity-disk`.
   Objects are visited in a fixed order; when two entity TYPES write the same
   key on one disk the first type's series is kept (disk guard).
4. `push.BatchPusher`: chunks of up to 100 objects / 1 MB, several in flight;
   a rejected chunk is re-sent per resource; ids that vanished are caught by a
   per-cycle verification sample.

### 2.5 Derived metrics (`app/push.py`)

Computed from the same object at the same sample timestamp, never zero-filled
(skipped if an input is missing or the denominator is zero):

| Key | Entity | Formula | Unit | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `cptvsan\|Network\|Physical NIC:{vmnic}\|rxLossRatio` | `vsan-pnic-net` | 100 x `rxMissErrRaw` / `rxPktRaw` | percent (by construction) | LIFETIME missed-packet share: both inputs are since-boot counters. The obvious `rxMissErr / rxPackets` was rejected because `rxMissErr` is already a ratio. |
| `cptvsan\|Storage I/O:dom-owner\|netSchedLowBandPct` | `host-domowner` | 100 x `numNetSchedLowBandSec` / (low + mid + high) | percent (by construction) | `numNetSchedHighBandSec` optional. None of the three inputs has a unit or a schema description in the repo's sources. |

## 3. entityRefId grammar per entity type

```
entityRefId := <entity-type> ":" <part0> [ "|" <part1> [ "|" <part2> ] ]
```

`<part0>` is always the scope uuid (cluster, vSAN node, disk, VM or virtual
disk). Arity can VARY within one type (host-cpu's per-host aggregate omits the
`cpu` part; `parse_ref` pads it with `(all)`).

*Identity source*: "live" = harvested from a real `VsanPerfQueryPerf` by
`tools/model_from_perfsvc.py` (names for the extra parts are hand-assigned in
that tool's `IDENTITY` dict); "**inferred**" = guessed from the entity NAME by
`tools/build_model_extra.py` `IDENTITY_RULES`, never observed. *Objects
observed* is from the model (lab of 4 hosts for the live types; 0 means never
seen). Example values are synthetic placeholders; where marked
"illustrative" the real format of that part is not recorded in the repo.

## 4. Ownership: which VMware object each entity lands on

### 4.1 Resolution rules (`app/push.py` `BUCKETS`, `app/perfsvc.py` `build_parent_map`)

The target kind is fixed per entity type by `TREE`. The MoRef is found by the
FIRST identity part, in this order, that the object carries:

| Target kind | Identity part -> bucket (first present wins) |
|---|---|
| ClusterComputeResource | `cluster_uuid` -> `clusters` |
| HostSystem | `host_uuid` -> `hosts`, `disk_uuid` -> `disks` |
| VirtualMachine | `vm_uuid` -> `vms`, `vdisk_uuid` -> `vdisks`, `disk_uuid` -> `vdisks` |

How each bucket is filled:

| Bucket | Source |
|---|---|
| `clusters` | vSAN cluster uuid -> cluster MoRef: `cluster.configurationEx.vsanConfigInfo.defaultConfig.uuid` |
| `hosts` | vSAN node uuid -> host MoRef: `host.configManager.vsanSystem.config.clusterInfo.nodeUuid`; fallback `VsanPerfQueryNodeInformation` hostname matched case-insensitively (FQDN or short name) to `host.name` |
| `disks` | vSAN disk uuid -> MoRef of the OWNING host: `QueryVsanManagedDisks(host)` storage-pool disks (ESA) first, then `host.configManager.vsanSystem.QueryDisksForVsan()` (`vsanUuid`, OSA and ESA) |
| `vms` | VM uuid -> VM MoRef: ContainerView of all VirtualMachines, keyed by BOTH `config.uuid` and `config.instanceUuid` |
| `vdisks` | datastore path -> VM MoRef: each VirtualDisk `backing.fileName` with the `[datastore] ` prefix stripped, registered with and without a leading `/` |

An object whose uuid is in no bucket is counted as *unmapped* (per entity type,
in the cycle's INFO summary line) and dropped -- nothing is ever pushed onto a
guess. Buckets cover only the clusters, hosts and VMs **visible in the vCenter
this adapter instance connects to**.

### 4.2 Ownership diagram

```
vCenter (VMEntityVCID = about.instanceUuid)
 |
 +-- ClusterComputeResource  <- cluster_uuid (vsanConfigInfo.defaultConfig.uuid)
 |     cptvsan|Capacity, Resync, Network|RDT, zDOM, PMEM, Data Protection,
 |     Storage I/O:<dom-client|dom-owner|dom-compmgr|remote-dom-client|
 |                  remote-dom-client-compute>,
 |     ESA Dedup:<dom-client-io|dom-owner-io|dom-compmgr-io|chunk-service|hash-service>
 |
 +-- HostSystem              <- host_uuid (vsanSystem...clusterInfo.nodeUuid)
 |     |                     <- disk_uuid (owning host of the disk)
 |     +-- per pNIC          Network|Physical NIC:<vmnic>
 |     +-- per vmknic        Network|VMkernel NIC:<vmk>   (vmknic stats + per-vmk RDT latency)
 |     +-- per TCP/IP stack  Network|TCP/IP Stack:<stack>
 |     +-- per disk          Disk:<disk_uuid>   (ESA layer, ESA SCSI/firmware, OSA cache,
 |     |                                         OSA capacity, DDH, CLOM disk)
 |     |                     Disk Group:<disk_uuid>
 |     +-- CPU               CPU:vsan, CPU:core-<cpu>, CPU:dom-<world>-<id>, CPU:lsom-<world>-<id>
 |     +-- Memory            Memory:vsan, Memory:system, Memory:heap-<heap>, Memory:slab-<slab>
 |     +-- iSCSI             iSCSI:host, iSCSI:target-<target>, iSCSI:lun-<target>-<lun>
 |     +-- variants          Storage I/O:<dom-client|dom-owner|dom-compmgr|remote-dom-client|
 |     |                                  remote-dom-client-compute|vsansparse>,
 |     |                     ESA Dedup:<component>, vSAN Direct:<host|cluster>
 |     +-- host-wide groups  Network|Host (host net + CMMDS + RDT net + RDT latency),
 |                           zDOM (top stats + VTX), Placement, PMEM, File Service, IOInsight
 |
 +-- VirtualMachine          <- vm_uuid (config.uuid or config.instanceUuid)
       |                     <- vdisk path (VirtualDisk backing.fileName)
       +-- VM:local, VM:remote                    (one each per VM)
       +-- per virtual disk  Virtual Disk:<path>, Virtual Disk:remote-<path>
       +-- per vSCSI device  vSCSI:<device>, vSCSI:remote-<device>
```

Note there is **no relationship** behind this diagram that the pack creates:
the cluster -> host -> VM drill-down is VMware's own inventory. A disk or NIC
is not an object; it is an instance on its host's metric keys.

### 4.4 External / remote clusters (vSAN HCI Mesh, compute-only clusters)

Every remote entity type is **provisional**: none has ever been observed
(`objects_observed = 0`), identity is inferred from the name, and metrics are
the advertised set from the schema. What the sources DO say:

| Entity type | Schema description (verbatim, first metric) | Lands on | Whose numbers (per the schema text) | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `cluster-remotedomclient` | Read IOPS consumed by all remote vSAN clients against the selected server cluster, such as virtual machines, etc. | cluster whose vSAN uuid is in the ref | SERVER side: IO that remote clients drive against this (server) cluster | **assumption**: that the ref's `cluster_uuid` is the server cluster |
| `computeCluster-remotedomclient` | Read IOPS consumed by all remote vSAN clients against the selected server cluster, such as virtual machines, etc. | cluster whose vSAN uuid is in the ref | **ambiguous**: the text is identical to cluster-remotedomclient's; the name suggests the CLIENT (compute-only) cluster's view | **assumption**; also unknown whether a compute-only cluster has a `vsanConfigInfo.defaultConfig.uuid` to resolve |
| `host-remotedomclient` | Read IOPS consumed by hosts in remote vSAN clients against the selected server cluster. | host whose vSAN node uuid is in the ref | per CLIENT host, against the server cluster | **assumption**: if the ref names a client host in another cluster the data lands on THAT host; if that host is managed by another vCenter it is unmapped and dropped |
| `computeHost-remotedomclient` | Read IOPS consumed by hosts in remote vSAN clients against the selected server cluster. | host whose vSAN node uuid is in the ref | **ambiguous**: same text as host-remotedomclient | **assumption**; whether a compute-only host exposes `clusterInfo.nodeUuid` is unverified (the hostname fallback via node information may apply) |
| `remote-virtual-machine` | Virtual machine read IOPS on remote datastore. | the VM (by uuid) | CLIENT side: the VM's IO to a remote datastore | **assumption** that `vm_uuid` matches `config.uuid`/`instanceUuid` like `virtual-machine` |
| `remote-virtual-disk` | This shows IOPS that are represented using a weighted size of 32KB by default on remote datastore. This means that a 64KB read or write operation represents 2 normalized IO. The weighted size is a configurable parameter. | the VM owning the disk path | CLIENT side, per virtual disk | **assumption**: the inferred part name is `disk_uuid` but it is resolved through the virtual-disk PATH map; if the ref carries a real disk uuid instead, every object is unmapped |
| `remote-vscsi` | Virtual machine VSCSI read IOPS on remote datastore. | the VM (by uuid) | CLIENT side, per vSCSI device | **assumption** (identity inferred) |

Consequences worth stating plainly:

* **Server and client numbers land in different places.** Server-side
  aggregates (`*-remotedomclient`) go on a cluster or host of the server
  cluster (as far as the inferred identity is right); client-side VM numbers go
  on the VM, wherever it runs. Nothing joins the two.
* **Cross-vCenter mesh is invisible.** All buckets come from the one vCenter
  the adapter instance connects to. A remote client or server in another
  vCenter is unmapped and dropped (counted in the cycle summary).
* **Possible double collection.** The adapter queries every cluster and merges
  results by `ObjectKey`. If the server cluster and the client cluster's
  queries both return the same ref, the later silently replaces the earlier
  before `plan` (this merge is not counted as a collision).
* **Remote and local share a group and differ by instance** (`VM:local` vs
  `VM:remote`, `Storage I/O:dom-client` vs `Storage I/O:remote-dom-client`,
  `vSCSI:<device>` vs `vSCSI:remote-<device>`). Whether `virtual-machine`
  ALSO includes a VM's remote IO (i.e. whether local + remote double-counts)
  is not established by any source here.
* Other "cluster" names that land on hosts: `vsan-direct-cluster`
  (inferred `host_uuid`). Other inferred identities that affect placement:
  `disk-group` (`disk_uuid`), `vsan-iscsi-*`, `heap-memory`, `slab-memory`,
  `lsom-world-cpu`.

## 5. The full cptvsan tree

Generated from `metric_tree.TREE`. A group with instances lists each instance template on its own line: `:{...}` is a DEVICE (one instance per value), a bare `:name` is a VARIANT (one per target; section 1). Metric counts are pushed metrics (after dropping `Actual` twins) plus derived. `[P]` = property (string), not a metric.

### Cluster (ClusterComputeResource)

```
cptvsan
  +-- Capacity    <- vsan-cluster-capacity (6)
  +-- Data Protection    <- vsan-dp-historical-stats (1)
  +-- ESA Dedup
    :chunk-service    <- cluster-esa-dedup-store-chunksvc (27)
    :dom-client-io    <- cluster-esa-dedup-domclient-io (24)
    :dom-compmgr-io    <- cluster-esa-dedup-domcompmgr-io (24)
    :dom-owner-io    <- cluster-esa-dedup-domowner-io (24)
    :hash-service    <- cluster-esa-dedup-store-hashsvc (34)
  +-- Network
    +-- RDT    <- cluster-rdt-network-latency (17)
  +-- PMEM    <- cluster-pmem (8)
  +-- Resync    <- cluster-resync (24)
  +-- Storage I/O
    :dom-client    <- cluster-domclient (14)
    :dom-compmgr    <- cluster-domcompmgr (15)
    :dom-owner    <- cluster-domowner (46)
    :remote-dom-client    <- cluster-remotedomclient (8)
    :remote-dom-client-compute    <- computeCluster-remotedomclient (8)
  +-- zDOM    <- cluster-zdom-top-stats (12)
  +-- Collector
    [P] lastUpdated
```

### Host (HostSystem)

```
cptvsan
  +-- CPU
    :core-{cpu}    <- host-cpu (3)
    :dom-{world_name}-{world_id}    <- dom-world-cpu (3)
    :lsom-{world_name}-{world_id}    <- lsom-world-cpu (2)
    :vsan    <- vsan-cpu (7)
  +-- Disk
    :{disk_uuid}    <- cache-disk (8), capacity-disk (13), clom-disk (3), ddh-disk (10), vsan-esa-disk-layer (38), vsan-esa-disk-scsifw (16)
    [P] name
    [P] firmware
    [P] model
    [P] vendor
    [P] driver
    [P] driver_version
  +-- Disk Group
    :{disk_uuid}    <- disk-group (38)
    [P] name
  +-- ESA Dedup
    :chunk-service    <- host-esa-dedup-store-chunksvc (27)
    :dom-client-io    <- host-esa-dedup-domclient-io (24)
    :dom-compmgr-io    <- host-esa-dedup-domcompmgr-io (24)
    :dom-owner-io    <- host-esa-dedup-domowner-io (24)
    :hash-service    <- host-esa-dedup-store-hashsvc (34)
  +-- File Service    <- vsan-file-service (8)
  +-- IOInsight    <- ioinsight (82)
  +-- Memory
    :heap-{heap_name}    <- heap-memory (1)
    :slab-{slab_name}    <- slab-memory (1)
    :system    <- system-mem (3)
    :vsan    <- vsan-memory (50)
  +-- Network
    +-- Host    <- cmmds-net (10), rdt-net (1), rdt-network-latency (17), vsan-host-net (16)
    +-- Physical NIC
      :{vmnic}    <- vsan-pnic-net (42)
    +-- TCP/IP Stack
      :{stack}    <- vsan-tcpip-stats (22)
    +-- VMkernel NIC
      :{vmknic}    <- vnic-rdt-network-latency (17), vsan-vnic-net (57)
  +-- PMEM    <- host-pmem (8)
  +-- Placement    <- clom-host (2), cmmds-workload (23), vsan-distribution (5)
  +-- Storage I/O
    :dom-client    <- host-domclient (78)
    :dom-compmgr    <- host-domcompmgr (82)
    :dom-owner    <- host-domowner (289)
    :remote-dom-client    <- host-remotedomclient (11)
    :remote-dom-client-compute    <- computeHost-remotedomclient (11)
    :vsansparse    <- host-vsansparse (21)
  +-- iSCSI
    :host    <- vsan-iscsi-host (10)
    :lun-{target}-{lun}    <- vsan-iscsi-lun (10)
    :target-{target}    <- vsan-iscsi-target (10)
  +-- vSAN Direct
    :cluster    <- vsan-direct-cluster (8)
    :host    <- vsan-direct-host (8)
  +-- zDOM    <- host-zdom-top-stats (19), zdom-vtx (71)
  +-- Collector
    [P] lastUpdated
```

### Virtual machine (VirtualMachine)

```
cptvsan
  +-- VM
    :local    <- virtual-machine (8)
    :remote    <- remote-virtual-machine (6)
  +-- Virtual Disk
    :remote-{disk_uuid}    <- remote-virtual-disk (3)
    :{vdisk_uuid}    <- virtual-disk (7)
    [P] name
  +-- vSCSI
    :remote-{device}    <- remote-vscsi (6)
    :{device}    <- vscsi (8)
  +-- Collector
    [P] lastUpdated
```

Properties: `name` is pushed only when the name map has a readable label different from the raw uuid/path (disk: `<model tokens> <serial> (<host name>)`; virtual disk: `<VM name> [<disk label>]`). Disk-hardware properties come from vCenter (`QueryDisksForVsan`, scsiTopology, HCL health summary), not from the Performance Service. `Disk:<disk_uuid>` is written by 6 entity types (`cache-disk`, `capacity-disk`, `clom-disk`, `ddh-disk`, `vsan-esa-disk-layer`, `vsan-esa-disk-scsifw`); `cache-disk`, `capacity-disk`, `vsan-esa-disk-layer`, `vsan-esa-disk-scsifw` carry these properties. An ESA disk is reported by both `vsan-esa-disk-layer` and `vsan-esa-disk-scsifw`, so on ESA every such property is written twice per disk per cycle (counted as a key collision). Metric-id overlaps inside one target are listed below.

### Keys two entity types would both write

Same target kind, group and instance template, same metric id -- so the same key. Computed from `TREE` and the model:

| Target | Key shape | Entity types | Shared metric ids |
|---|---|---|---|
| HostSystem | `cptvsan\|Disk:{disk_uuid}\|<metric>` | `cache-disk`, `capacity-disk`, `vsan-esa-disk-scsifw` | 8: `iopsDevRead`, `iopsDevWrite`, `latencyDevDAvg`, `latencyDevGAvg`, `latencyDevRead`, `latencyDevWrite`, `throughputDevRead`, `throughputDevWrite` |

The `Disk` overlap is between OSA cache / capacity disks and ESA disks. They are written under different disk uuids (an OSA disk is a cache disk or a capacity disk, and is not an ESA disk), so no single key receives two values; but nothing in the key says which architecture or role a disk has.

### Reserved groups

Top-level names held in `metric_tree.RESERVED` with **no data source yet**. Nothing is collected or pushed under them; they are reserved so that a future source lands on a stable key instead of forcing a rename (section 10, history continuity).

| Reserved group | Why there is no source |
|---|---|
| `cptvsan\|Fibre Channel` | no vSAN Performance Service entity type in the captured schema |
| `cptvsan\|Object Store` | no vSAN Performance Service entity type (S3 / Data Persistence) in the captured schema |

## 6. Units of measure

### 6.1 The authorities, and the precedence between them

There are two outside authorities for a unit, and they disagree:

1. **HCIBench** -- units harvested once from HCIBench's Grafana dashboards
   (which chart the same vSAN stats) and committed as
   `docs/assets/hcibench_units.json` (1523 metric ids). Mapped to SDK
   unit paths by `tools/build_metric_labels.py` `UNIT_MAP`.
2. **The vSAN Performance Service schema** -- `docs/assets/perfsvc_schema.json`
   (`unit` per metric: `time_ms`, `rate_bytes`, `permille`, `number`, ...).
   **Not used for units**, because the schema's `unit` hangs off the GRAPH
   the metric is charted on, not the metric itself, and a graph mixes metric
   kinds. It describes an axis, not a series. Bulk-applying it would put a
   1000x error on every DOM latency (section 6.6). Its NAMES are used, as
   labels.

`tools/build_metric_labels.py` writes `app/metric_labels.py` `UNITS` with this
precedence (per metric id, globally -- NOT per entity type):

1. `UNIT_OVERRIDE` (hand-set, each with cited evidence) wins.
2. else the HCIBench unit, if it maps to an SDK unit (an `*Actual` twin
   inherits its base metric's HCIBench unit);
3. else a conservative **name rule** (`_unit_from_name`): suffix `Ms` -> ms;
   `Us`/`USec` -> µs; contains `latency` or ends `lat` -> µs; `throughput` ->
   B/s; `iops` -> IOPS; ends `bytes` -> bytes; ends `pct`/`percent`/`util` -> %;
4. metrics in `perfsvc.PERMILLE` start as `RATIO.PERCENT` (the adapter divides
   them by 10), but steps 1-3 overwrite that when they yield a unit;
5. `AMBIGUOUS_UNIT` metrics get no unit, deliberately.

"A wrong unit is worse than none, because Operations will scale and render it
confidently" -- so anything unresolved stays unitless.

Distinct metric ids by the authority their final unit came from (reconstructed
by this generator and checked against `app/metric_labels.py`):

### 6.2 HCIBench unit vocabulary

| HCIBench unit | SDK unit path | Symbol | Metric ids whose unit came from it |
|---|---|---|---|
| `binBps` | `DATA_RATE.BIBYTE_PER_SECOND` | B/s | 63 |
| `Bps` | `DATA_RATE.BYTE_PER_SECOND` | B/s | 0 |
| `bytes` | `DATA_SIZE.BYTE` | bytes | 38 |
| `mbytes` | `DATA_SIZE.MEGABYTE` | MB | 1 |
| `µs` | `TIME.MICROSECONDS` | µs | 85 |
| `us` | `TIME.MICROSECONDS` | µs | 0 |
| `ns` | `TIME.NANOSECONDS` | ns | 2 |
| `ms` | `TIME.MILLISECONDS` | ms | 13 |
| `s` | `TIME.SECONDS` | s | 5 |
| `iops` | `MISC.IO_OPERATIONS_PER_SECOND` | IOPS | 61 |
| `percent` | `RATIO.PERCENT` | % | 19 |
| `percentunit` | `RATIO.PERCENT` | % | 1 |
| `short` | (dimensionless) | - | 0 |
| `none` | (dimensionless) | - | 0 |

HCIBench units present for our metrics but NOT in `UNIT_MAP` (therefore
ignored, the name rule applied instead): `KBps` (1).

### 6.3 Hand overrides (`UNIT_OVERRIDE`)

| Metric | Unit |
|---|---|
| `iopsCacheMissRate` | `RATIO.PERCENT` |
| `tputCacheMissRate` | `RATIO.PERCENT` |
| `checkpointWorkerWakeupMs` | `TIME.MILLISECONDS` |
| `total` | `DATA_SIZE.BYTE` |
| `free` | `DATA_SIZE.BYTE` |
| `used` | `DATA_SIZE.BYTE` |
| `savedByDedup` | `DATA_SIZE.BYTE` |
| `totalDpOverhead` | `DATA_SIZE.BYTE` |

Evidence, per the comments in `tools/build_metric_labels.py`: the two cache-miss
rates are ratios (official names "Cache Miss Per IOPS/Throughput", schema unit
`percentage`); `checkpointWorkerWakeupMs` reads a constant 20000, a 20 s wakeup,
which HCIBench charts in µs; the five capacity values are raw bytes for which
the schema-derived path declared no unit.

### 6.4 Per-mille: converted to percent at collection

`app/perfsvc.py` `PERMILLE` -- 24 metric ids divided by 10 on
collection and declared `RATIO.PERCENT`. Note that the conversion is keyed by
metric id ALONE, so it also applies on entity types where the schema gives the
metric no unit (e.g. the `tcp*` ids on `vsan-vnic-net`) -- that those are also
per-mille is an **assumption**. The SDK has no per-mille unit; the deliberate
trade is that the pushed number differs from the vSphere UI (which shows
per-mille).

### 6.6 Adjudicated findings: units we believe the schema gets WRONG (and the ones still open)

Restated from the maintainer's internal analysis (purged from this public
repository): metric, our unit, schema unit, the value actually observed on a
lab cluster, the verdict and why. Embedded as data in
`tools/build_scotts_readme.py` (`ADJUDICATED`).

| Metric / family | Ours | Schema | Observed | Verdict | Reasoning |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| latencyAvgRead (and the latencyAvg* / latencyStddev* / latAvg* family) | TIME.MICROSECONDS | time_ms | 589 and 808 on cluster-domclient; 593 on cluster-zdom-top-stats beside readLatencyMaxUs 565,482 | **ours (µs)** | 589 ms on an idle NVMe cluster is absurd; 0.59 ms is right. A sibling metric explicitly in µs sits in the same range. |
| rxSbSpaceMin | DATA_SIZE.BYTE | rate_bytes | 8,286,980 | **ours (bytes)** | An 8 MB socket buffer, not 8 MB/s. The schema's own name ('RDT Network Socket Min Inbound Bytes') says bytes and contradicts its graph unit. |
| portRxDrops | RATIO.PERCENT (÷10) | permille | 0,0,1,1 on one series; a 12-sample series rose and fell | **schema accepted, unproven** | First read as counts (small integers). Non-monotonic, so not a counter; official name 'Inbound Packet Drop Rate ...', description 'percentage of'. Now treated as per-mille and converted. No non-zero cross-check. |
| pauseCount | RATIO.PERCENT (÷10) | permille | 0 in every sample | **schema probably right, unproven** | Id says count; official name 'pNic 802.3x Pause Rate', described as a percentage. Cannot be settled by measurement while every value is 0. |
| tcpRcvdupackRate | RATIO.PERCENT (÷10) | permille | 1-2 from perfsvc; an independent host-side scrape measured 0.122 % for the same quantity | **schema right (per-mille)** | The value agrees with per-mille, not percent. This is the evidence the 24-metric PERMILLE conversion rests on. |
| rxMissErr vs rxMissErrRaw | RATIO.PERCENT (÷10) vs none | permille | base 0.0 while its Raw twin read 43,429 on the same NIC | **different quantities** | The base is a per-mille RATE over the sample interval; *Raw is the cumulative count since the NIC last reset. Neither is a duplicate. |
| *Raw (rxPktRaw, txPktRaw, pauseCountRaw) | none | (varies) | strictly rising / step-only over 13 samples | **cumulative counters** | The ONLY monotonic series found among 194 classified; pushed as plain gauges, so they plot as an ever-rising line. |
| 74 other schema-permille metrics (readRatio, aligned4kRatio, randomRatio, riosz*, ...) | none (not converted) | permille | 0.0 everywhere observed | **open** | The schema was right every time it was checked, which argues for converting all of them; but no non-zero value exists to verify, so they are left unconverted and unitless rather than declared %. |
| tput* / throughput family | DATA_RATE.BIBYTE_PER_SECOND | rate_bytes | a capacity-disk write rate of 664,576 on an idle disk; a compression throughput of 3,197,256 | **bytes/s (agrees with schema)** | Refutes a third-party catalogue that guessed KB/s: 650 KB/s idle is plausible, 650 MB/s is not. |
| checkpointWorkerWakeupMs | TIME.MILLISECONDS | (none) | constant 20000 | **ms (overrides HCIBench µs)** | A 20 s periodic wakeup; as µs it would be 20 ms. The id suffix says ms. |
| iopsCacheMissRate / tputCacheMissRate | RATIO.PERCENT | percentage | - | **percent (overrides name rule)** | Official names 'Cache Miss Per IOPS/Throughput' and the schema unit agree it is a ratio, not an IOPS or throughput figure. |
| dedupRatio | none | percentage | 132 where (used + saved) / used = 1.326 | **neither: ratio x 100** | 132 means 1.32:1, not 132 %. Operations has no unit for this, so the scale is put in the label: 'Space Efficiency Ratio (100 = 1.00x)'. |
| total / free / used / savedByDedup / totalDpOverhead | DATA_SIZE.BYTE | size_bytes (none for totalDpOverhead) | 14-digit raw byte counts | **bytes (agrees)** | Declared so views can ask for TiB/GiB. |
| avgPctTimeToPeak | none | (none) | - | **open** | HCIBench charts it in µs, the id says percent; nothing adjudicates. |
| tcpErrs | RATIO.PERCENT (÷10) | permille (tcpip-stats) | 0 | **contradiction in our own code** | perfsvc.PERMILLE divides it by 10 and the unit says %, while tools/build_view.py lists it in CUMULATIVE and appends '(Total)' to its column label. It cannot be both a since-boot total and a per-mille ratio; which is right is not established. |

General conclusion recorded there: the schema is wrong for **latency**
(`time_ms` where values are µs) and for at least one **socket-buffer size**
(`rate_bytes` where it is bytes); it has been RIGHT every time its `permille`
was checked against an independent measurement. Adjudication should prefer
the official *description* over the graph unit, and needs non-zero observed
values.

### 6.7 Metrics whose unit is still unknown -- open questions

583 of 989 distinct metric ids
(793 pushed entity/metric pairs) have **no unit** in our table.
Many are genuinely dimensionless counts; the rest are unknown. Suggestions
with evidence (a documented unit, or a non-zero value with an obvious scale)
are wanted. Note in particular the ESA disk-layer latencies
`avgLatReadCapacity`, `avgLatWriteCapacity`, `avgLatReadPerf`,
`avgLatWritePerf`, `avgLatUnmapCapacity`, `avgLatUnmapPerf`: HCIBench has no
entry, the name rule does not match `avgLat*`, and the schema says `time_ms`
for two of them --
which, by the latency finding above, is probably µs, but is unproven.

## 7. Complete metric catalog

Every modelled metric of every entity type (1636 (entity, metric) pairs,
41 of them `Actual` twins dropped at collection) plus the
2 derived metrics. Grouped by target kind, then tree
group, then entity type.

Columns: **Key** -- the full pushed key with the instance template in braces;
**Label** -- `app/metric_labels.py` LABELS (source: *override* = hand-set,
*schema* = Broadcom's official name from `perfsvc_schema.json`, *derived* = a
mechanical expansion of the id, NOT an authoritative meaning); **Unit** -- SDK
unit path from `app/metric_labels.py` UNITS, or "unit unknown";
**Authority** -- where that unit came from (section 6); **Schema unit** -- the
Performance Service schema's graph unit, shown for comparison only, "-" when
the schema has no entry for this (entity, metric); **V** -- referenced by a
shipped view (`app/metric_scope.py`); **Notes** -- `dropped` (Actual twin, not
collected), `÷10` (per-mille converted), `cumulative` (`*Raw`, since-boot
counter), `derived`.

Labels and units are per metric id across all entity types; the same id on two
entity types always gets the same label and unit here.

### 7.1 Cluster (ClusterComputeResource)

## 8. Collection scope and cost

* **Default: everything.** `perfsvc.collect(scope=None)` queries every
  modelled entity type and keeps every metric (minus `Actual` twins). The
  adapter passes no scope. (`app/metric_scope.py`, a list of metrics
  referenced by shipped views, still exists but the 2.0.0 adapter does not use
  it.)
* **One `VsanPerfQueryPerf` per entity type per cluster** -- 69
  round trips per cluster per full pass (CORE types every cycle, HEAVY types
  every 3rd; see "Collection cadence" below). The service returns every metric of an
  entity type in one call, so narrowing the metric set saves Operations
  storage, never a round trip; only skipping whole entity types saves time.
  (`VsanPerfQuerySpec.labels` filters server-side; the adapter sends it when
  `query_labels` is `true`, the default.) Measured on a single-cluster,
  four-host lab: a cycle took about 4.7 s and made about 34 vCenter calls;
  vCenter serialises vSAN performance queries at roughly 7 calls per second,
  so cycle time scales with the number of clusters per vCenter (about 30
  calls per cluster per cycle). A projection, not a measurement: about 110 s
  for 25 clusters on one vCenter.
* **Feature-specific entity types** (`perfsvc.FEATURE_ENTITIES`,
  26 types) return nothing, or fault `NotSupported` /
  `InvalidArgument`, when the feature is off. They are QUERIED by default so a
  feature enabled later reports with no config change. The adapter instance
  option **Feature-specific entity types = `skip`** (`feature_entity_types`)
  leaves them out, saving 26 round trips per cluster per cycle.

| Feature | Entity types |
|---|---|
| ESA global deduplication | `cluster-esa-dedup-domclient-io`, `cluster-esa-dedup-domcompmgr-io`, `cluster-esa-dedup-domowner-io`, `cluster-esa-dedup-store-chunksvc`, `cluster-esa-dedup-store-hashsvc`, `host-esa-dedup-domclient-io`, `host-esa-dedup-domcompmgr-io`, `host-esa-dedup-domowner-io`, `host-esa-dedup-store-chunksvc`, `host-esa-dedup-store-hashsvc` |
| iSCSI target service | `vsan-iscsi-host`, `vsan-iscsi-lun`, `vsan-iscsi-target` |
| PMEM | `cluster-pmem`, `host-pmem` |
| File service | `vsan-file-service` |
| vSAN Direct | `vsan-direct-cluster`, `vsan-direct-host` |
| IOInsight | `ioinsight` |
| Remote datastore / compute-only (HCI Mesh) | `cluster-remotedomclient`, `computeCluster-remotedomclient`, `computeHost-remotedomclient`, `host-remotedomclient`, `remote-virtual-disk`, `remote-virtual-machine`, `remote-vscsi` |

* **Silent but not feature-gated.** These types were never observed
  (`objects_observed = 0`) and are NOT in `FEATURE_ENTITIES`, so `skip` does
  not remove them -- chiefly the OSA families, which are silent on ESA:
  `cache-disk`, `capacity-disk`, `clom-disk`, `clom-host`, `cluster-resync`, `cmmds-workload`, `ddh-disk`, `disk-group`, `heap-memory`, `lsom-world-cpu`, `slab-memory`, `vsan-distribution`.
* **Per cycle** in Operations: batch stats and properties POSTs (up to 100
  objects or 1 MB each by default), plus a paged `GET api/resources` listing
  per VMware kind only when the id cache expires.
* **Volume.** On the lab the live model was harvested from, host-scoped
  per-instance types dominate: `dom-world-cpu` 315
  objects, `host-cpu` 244, `vscsi`
  106, `virtual-disk`
  60 (4 hosts, 30 VMs). `host-domowner`
  alone has 288 metrics per host.

### Collection cadence

2.0.0 splits collection in two. **CORE** entity types
(56 of the 69 in `TREE`) are
queried every 5-minute cycle. **HEAVY** types (13) are
rotated: each is queried every 3rd cycle, over a 15-minute backfill window, and
every 5-minute sample in that window is pushed with **its own timestamp**
(the window starts at the newest sample already pushed and reaches back at most
`max_backfill_minutes`, default 60, after an outage). So
the time series of a HEAVY metric has the same 5-minute resolution as a CORE
one; it simply arrives up to 15 minutes later. A HEAVY type therefore costs one
round trip per cluster per 15 minutes instead of three. The buckets are
assigned deterministically by nominal weight (`cadence.assign_buckets`) so
each cycle carries about the same load.

**Skipping what is empty or unsupported.** A type that returned no entity for a
cluster (a feature not in use) is remembered and not queried again for
`empty_recheck_minutes` (default 60; 0 queries every type every cycle). The
memory is dropped at once when a host or disk is added to or removed from the
cluster. `cluster-resync` is the exception: its entities exist only while a
resync runs, so it is always probed. A type the cluster does not support
(`NotSupported` / `InvalidArgument`) is remembered for 6 hours.

**Protecting vCenter.** At most `vcenter_concurrency` calls (default 4) are in
flight per vCenter, shared by every adapter instance in the same collector
container that collects it. The limit is halved when vCenter faults or slows
down and raised one step after that many clean calls. Cluster queries start
spread over `stagger_seconds` (default 30), at most `cluster_concurrency`
clusters at once. Responses are requested gzip-compressed (`vcenter_gzip`).
Inventory is read incrementally with `WaitForUpdatesEx` and in full once an
hour.

| HEAVY family | Entity types |
|---|---|
| per-core / per-world CPU | `host-cpu`, `dom-world-cpu`, `lsom-world-cpu` |
| vSCSI | `vscsi`, `remote-vscsi` |
| heap / slab memory | `heap-memory`, `slab-memory` |
| placement | `clom-host`, `cmmds-workload`, `vsan-distribution` |
| DDH / CLOM disk | `ddh-disk`, `clom-disk` |
| IOInsight | `ioinsight` |


## 9. Known gaps, conflicts and open questions

Each item is phrased as a question; suggestions are the point of this document.

### 9.1 Tree shape

1. **Disk instances are UUIDs, not friendly names.** `Disk:<disk_uuid>`
   etc. A readable `name` property is pushed per instance when resolvable,
   but the KEY carries the uuid. Should the instance be a stable friendly
   name (canonical device name? serial?) -- and what is stable enough to keep
   history across a disk replacement or a host rename?
2. **CPU aggregate instance.** `host-cpu` returns a per-host aggregate
   with no `cpu` part, which becomes `cptvsan|CPU:cores-total` alongside
   `cptvsan|CPU:core-cpu-8` ... Should the aggregate be its own variant (e.g.
   `CPU:cores-total`) instead of a magic device value?
3. **`/` and other characters in instances and group names.** Sanitising only
   replaces `:` and `|`. Virtual-disk instances contain `/`
   (`<namespace-uuid>/<file>.vmdk`), iSCSI IQNs lose their colons, and two
   group names contain `/` (`Network|TCP/IP Stack`, `Storage I/O`). Is
   `/` safe in Operations metric keys and in content-factory key handling?
   Should virtual-disk instances use the VM's disk label (`Hard disk 1`)?
4. **One `Disk` group for every architecture and role.** 6 entity
   types write `Disk:<disk_uuid>` (section 5). The key no longer says whether a
   disk is ESA, OSA cache or OSA capacity, and OSA disks and ESA disks share
   8 device-layer metric ids. Should architecture and role be a
   pushed property of the disk instance, so content can filter on it? And
   should `Disk Group` (kept separate, keyed by an inferred `disk_uuid`) stay a
   group of its own?
5. **Variant and device instances share one syntax.** `Storage I/O:dom-owner`
   (a variant) and `Network|Physical NIC:vmnic0` (a device) look alike. A view
   about one variant must bind it by name; a device view uses the `*`
   wildcard, which also matches a sibling's prefixed devices where metric ids
   coincide (`vSCSI:remote-<device>` rows appear in a `vSCSI:*` view). Is a
   prefix convention (`core-`, `remote-`, `heap-`) enough for content-factory,
   or should variants be a separate key level?
6. **Cluster vs host duplication.** `ESA Dedup`, `PMEM`, `Storage I/O`, `zDOM` exist with the same group
   name on both the cluster and the host. Is identical naming across kinds
   what content-factory wants (one widget definition, two subject kinds), or
   should scope be explicit in the key?
7. **Merged non-instanced groups.** `Network|Host` (HostSystem), `Placement` (HostSystem), `zDOM` (HostSystem) each hold several entity
   types with no instance to tell them apart. Safe only while their metric ids
   stay disjoint -- see "Keys two entity types would both write" in section 5.
   A future schema adding a shared id would silently interleave two series.
8. **`vsan-vnic-net` drops its `stack` part.** Instance is `{vmknic}` only;
   the ref carries `host_uuid|stack|vmknic`. Safe while a vmknic belongs to one
   stack. Keep?
9. **Cluster-sounding variant on a host.** `vsan-direct-cluster` lands on the
   HOST as `vSAN Direct:cluster`, beside `vSAN Direct:host`. Is that name
   misleading?
10. **Reserved groups.** `Fibre Channel`, `Object Store` are reserved with no source (section 5).
    Are these the right names for content-factory to plan around?

### 9.2 Units and semantics

11. 583 distinct metric ids have no unit (section 6.7). Which can be
    settled?
12. Should the 74 unconverted schema-`permille` metrics be converted too
    (section 6.4/6.6)?
13. `tcpErrs` is divided by 10 as a per-mille ratio **and** labelled a
    cumulative "(Total)" by the view generator. Which is it?
14. `*Raw` metrics are cumulative since-boot counters pushed as gauges. Push a
    derived per-interval delta, or leave them?
15. `dedupRatio` is a ratio x 100 with no unit that can express it. Push a
    derived true ratio instead?
16. Units are global per metric id, not per (entity, metric). Is any id used
    with different meanings on different entity types?

### 9.3 Attribution and identity

17. The 38 provisional entity types have INFERRED identity
    (section 3). Any first-hand knowledge of their real `entityRefId` shapes --
    especially `disk-group`, `remote-virtual-disk`, `vsan-direct-cluster`,
    `vsan-iscsi-*`, `heap-memory`, `slab-memory` -- would correct placement.
18. Remote/compute-only attribution (section 4.4): which cluster does each
    `*-remotedomclient` ref name -- client or server?

### 9.4 Contradictions found in the repository's own sources

* `app/perfsvc_model.py`'s docstring says "39 of 70 entity types return
  nothing"; the merged model has 69 types, 38 of them
  never observed.
* `perfsvc.metrics_for` cites "201 such pairs" of `Actual` twins (counted
  across objects); the model has 41 (entity, metric) `Actual` twins.

## 10. How to propose a change

* **One source.** `app/metric_tree.py` (`TREE`, `PREFIX`, `build_key`,
  `sanitize_instance`) is the ONLY place a key is shaped. `app/push.py` builds
  every pushed key through it; `tools/build_view.py` derives every view
  column's key from it (and fails the build if its own instance-wildcard form
  ever disagrees with `build_key`); a view about one VARIANT binds that
  instance by name (`Storage I/O:dom-owner`), a DEVICE view uses the `*`
  wildcard. Help text (`where_it_lives`) is derived from it too. Propose changes as edits to `TREE` rows (target kind, group
  path, instance template) or to `sanitize_instance`.
* **Cost: history continuity.** A key is the identity of a time series in
  Operations. Renaming a group, changing an instance template, or changing
  sanitising starts a NEW series; the old one stops receiving data and its
  history is not carried over. There are no Operations objects of ours to
  migrate -- only keys on VMware objects -- so the trade is purely history
  vs. a better tree. 2.0.0 is the first release with this tree, so now is the
  cheapest time.
* **Content regenerates.** Views are generated by `tools/build_view.py` from
  `TREE`; dashboards and reports in `content/` reference those views; the
  metric scope list (`app/metric_scope.py`, via `tools/gen_metric_scope.py`)
  and help (`tools/build_help_html.py`) derive from the same data. After a
  tree change: regenerate views/dashboards, then run the offline tests
  (`test_perfsvc.py`, `test_push.py`, `test_content.py`, `test_adapter.py`).
  `test_push.py` asserts every modelled entity type is in `TREE` and that every
  `TREE` row keys cleanly from its identity with exactly the levels its group
  declares (an instance value must never add a level).
* **Metrics referenced by shipped content** are marked **V** in section 7;
  changing their keys requires the content regeneration above.
* **Hard constraints that any proposal must keep** (`CLAUDE.md`): no object
  kinds of our own, no relationships, nothing written onto any object except
  through the stats/properties endpoints under `cptvsan|`.
* **Verify the outcome, not the code path.** A proposed change should state
  the one measurement that proves it (e.g. "every HostSystem in cluster X has
  `cptvsan|Network|Physical NIC:<vmnic>|rxMissErr` for each vSAN uplink,
  and `cptvsan|Collector|lastUpdated` advanced this cycle").
## 11. Coverage gaps by vSAN feature

For each vSAN feature an operator might expect to monitor, this section states
which Performance Service entity types relate to it, whether we model them,
whether they were ever observed returning data, and what is missing. It is
written to be argued with.

**Method and vocabulary.**

* *Related entity types* are chosen by hand, from a term search over four
  sources, all printed per area so the choice can be checked:
  the schema `docs/assets/perfsvc_schema.json` (captured from
  `VsanPerfGetSupportedEntityTypes`; entity name, metric id, name,
  description), our model `app/perfsvc_model*.py` (metric ids returned but not
  advertised), the HCIBench vocabulary `docs/assets/hcibench_units.json`
  (metric ids its Grafana dashboards chart -- the ENTITY TYPE behind those ids
  is not recorded, so a hit there says only that some source emits such an id),
  and the method names of the vendored vSAN Management API bindings
  `docs/assets/vsan_api_methods.json` (198 methods; header: "Copyright 2020-2021 VMware, Inc.").
* *Relation*: **direct** = the entity name or its schema text names the
  feature; *inference* = related by reasoning stated in the row, not by the
  sources.
* *Status*: "observed (n objects)" = `ENTITIES[...]['objects_observed'] > 0`
  in the live model; "modelled, never observed (keys unverified)" =
  `objects_observed == 0`: the type is queried and keyed from the schema, but
  its identity, metric set and keys have never been checked against data.
* Where no entity type relates directly, the subsection says exactly "No Performance Service entity type in the captured schema covers this."
  That is a statement about this captured schema (one cluster, one version),
  **not** a claim that vSAN lacks the capability.
* A vendored method name is evidence that the binding declares it, nothing
  more: whether the target vCenter serves it, and what it returns, is not
  verified anywhere in this repository. INFERENCE: the bindings
  predate ESA-era features, so the absence of a name there is weak evidence.
* "Open question" lines name where data MIGHT come from. None is asserted to
  exist.

**Group A -- Data services and access protocols**

### 11.1 vSAN File Services (NFS / SMB file shares)

*Term search* `\bfile|\bnfs\b|\bsmb\b|\bshares?\b|protocol` (case-insensitive; raw hits, relevance is judged in the tables, not by the hit):

* schema text (entity name, metric id, name, description): `vsan-file-service` (8)
* model ids NOT advertised by the schema: no hits
* HCIBench ids not in our model: 0
* vendored vSAN Management API methods matching `File|FsDomain|Fsvm`: `HostVsanHealthSystem.VsanHostQueryFileServiceHealthSummary`, `VsanFileServiceSystem.VsanClusterCreateFsDomain`, `VsanFileServiceSystem.VsanClusterQueryFileShares`, `VsanFileServiceSystem.VsanClusterQueryFsDomains`, `VsanFileServiceSystem.VsanClusterReconfigureFsDomain`, `VsanFileServiceSystem.VsanClusterRemoveFsDomain`, `VsanFileServiceSystem.VsanCreateFileShare`, `VsanFileServiceSystem.VsanDownloadFileServiceOvf`, `VsanFileServiceSystem.VsanPerformFileServiceEnablePreflightCheck`, `VsanFileServiceSystem.VsanQueryFileServiceOvfs`, `VsanFileServiceSystem.VsanReconfigureFileShare`, `VsanFileServiceSystem.VsanUpgradeFsvm`, `VsanVcClusterHealthSystem.VsanClusterQueryFileServiceHealthSummary`, `VsanVcClusterHealthSystem.VsanDownloadHclFile_Task`, `VsanVcClusterHealthSystem.VsanPurgeHclFiles`

| Entity type | Relation | Why related | Target and cptvsan group | Model | Status | Schema metrics | Modelled metrics | Feature-gated | Schema description (first metric) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `vsan-file-service` | direct | schema text: file-service IOPS / bytes / latency | HostSystem `cptvsan\|File Service` | provisional | modelled, never observed (keys unverified) | 8 | 8 | yes | IOPS for read operations. |

**Gap / risk**

* Eight aggregate metrics only (read/write ops, requested vs transferred bytes, latency). The schema text never mentions NFS, SMB, a share, a protocol or a client (computed: 0 of its metrics' name/description/graph text match nfs, smb, share, protocol or client), so per-share, per-protocol and per-file-server breakdowns are absent from the captured schema.
* Identity is INFERRED as `host_uuid` (the fallback rule in `tools/build_model_extra.py`), and the TREE row has no instance. If the service returns more than one object per host (e.g. per file server or per share -- unknown), they share one key on the HostSystem: `push.plan` counts a collision and the last object written wins.
* QUICKSTART.md lists file services as "present in the schema, never enabled".

**Open question -- where else the data might come from** (not asserted to exist): Per-share and file-service health: the vendored bindings declare `VsanClusterQueryFileShares`, `VsanClusterQueryFileServiceHealthSummary` and `VsanHostQueryFileServiceHealthSummary` (listed above). Whether they return per-share usage or performance is not established here.

| Entity type | Relation | Why related | Target and cptvsan group | Model | Status | Schema metrics | Modelled metrics | Feature-gated | Schema description (first metric) |

**Gap / risk**

* Never observed. The schema text says every metric is per VMDK, but the identity is INFERRED as `host_uuid` (fallback rule) and the TREE row has no instance. If the service returns one object per VMDK under a host uuid, all of a host's VMDKs collide on one key (counted as collisions, last wins); if the first part is not a host uuid, every object is unmapped. Either way the per-VMDK value is lost. This is the clearest placement defect in the inferred model.
* 74 of its 82 metrics are schema-`permille`; 0 of those are converted by `perfsvc.PERMILLE` (section 6.4).
* IOInsight runs only while an IOInsight instance is active (inference), so it is correctly feature-gated in `FEATURE_ENTITIES`.

**Open question -- where else the data might come from** (not asserted to exist): Should IOInsight land on the VirtualMachine with a `{vdisk}` instance, like `virtual-disk`? Needs one observed ref.
